From an ext4 clone the path is either a `\\wsl.localhost` UNC path, where dotnet can't write its obj state files (MSB3101, then MSB3030), or a plain `\home\...` path that doesn't exist (MSB1009). It only works from the `/mnt/d` tree.
- **Fix:** a new shared helper, `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\Utils\selftest_winstage.py`. From an ext4 clone it builds through `winbuild.stage_build` under `D:\Luke\dev\_rmbuild`, with one stage per csproj so parallel runs don't collide. The stage includes the whole `src` tree because these tests walk up from their exe to read shipped defs. On the `/mnt` tree it behaves as before.
- **Edits:** each of the seven selftests now calls the helper in place of `_to_windows_path(CSPROJ)`.
- **First run:** the first run of each test is slow while the roughly 319M `src` tree is rsynced (all eight together ran for over 10 minutes); later runs are incremental. Each test keeps its own copy of that tree on D:, so expect about 2 GB there.

<result>Part 2 is built offline and pushed at `4764c7143`, with 139/139 selftests passing. The item is marked implemented; the two live checks (settings screen loads, pulse timing on a big network) are still owed.

**What was wrong:** a canal network bigger than 6,000 cells is worked in fixed pieces, and liquid never crossed the border between pieces, so the far parts stayed dry for good.

**What changed:**
- **Moving borders:** when a network is bigger than the limit, the border between pieces now moves every pulse. All pieces read one flow order, worked out once for the whole network. My first try gave each piece its own order, and that failed: a piece with no pond or river in it never spread. Networks under the limit run exactly as before.
- **Two new Mod Settings:** a slider for the piece size (`maxNetworkCells`, 1,000–50,000, default 6,000, same as shipped) and a toggle for the moving borders (`networkSeamsMoveEnabled`, on by default).
- **Kernel fixture:** a 200-cell channel with the limit set to 50 wets all 200 cells with moving borders and only 49 with fixed borders, then settles with the liquid totals balanced. A network under the limit gives an identical result pulse for pulse.

**Trade-off:** moving borders fill a network more slowly than working it in one piece. After 400 pulses, 136 of 200 cells were wet, against 200 for one uncapped piece. That's why raising the default limit still matters, and it waits on the in-game timing.

**Loose ends:**
- The ledger event is still only in `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry`, in the uncommitted `infrastructure/state/ledger/events/FOUNDRY.jsonl`. I didn't commit it because that clone already had other people's uncommitted work.
- The C# selftest can't build from an ext4 clone, so I ran it from a staged copy on `D:\Luke\dev\_rmscratch\flowpart2`.
- I didn't run `selftest_flowworks_kernel_oracle.py`. Its test networks are at most 144 cells and the new mode is off in the kernel by default, so it shouldn't be affected.

It never takes animals, pawns, plants, worn or carried gear, or holograms. Release puts back exactly the colour override the lamp had, so a lamp with no override gets none back. Where a scenario decides for a map, it hides the buttons through a code call. Mod Settings has two switches: the light channel, and the buttons. - **Script:** `validation.py` has static checks plus a live proof that leaves the map as it found it. The C# selftest can only test the colour fields, not the live repaint path, because a plain .NET process can't run the game's colour-write method. That path is first tested by the live proof.

<result>FLOWWORKS_QUARRY_DIGGING_1 river half (sluice box and panning) is built and pushed at `e569931e6`, and marked `implemented` with five criteria still to prove live (one L1, four L2). None of the new parts has run in a game yet, and nothing is deployed.

**What exists now:**
- **Sluice box** (`RM_SluiceBox`): a 1×2 building with one end in shallow moving river water and one on the bank. Once a day it washes a little ore onto its bank end. Calm water and still water give nothing.
- **Panning spot** (`RM_PanningSpot`): goes on the dry bank beside shallow moving water. Panning is Mining work. A colonist pans for about two hours, gains a little Mining XP, and usually finds nothing, sometimes 1–3 units.
- **Shared stock:** sluices and panners draw on one stock per map. It regrows over a year and a flood refills it.
- **Mod Settings:** six controls in FlowWorks' Mod Settings: three switches (sluice, panning, flood refill) and three sliders (stock size, sluice yield, panning yield).
- **Test script:** a new `placer` check in the rivers test suite builds its own sluice box, forces washes, then cleans up after itself. I also added a review-map station for panning.

The C# builds clean, the FlowWorks selftests pass 140/140, and the rivers, northstar and review-map checks pass. The full selftest run was red. One failure was mine: adding the sluice and spot defs made the review map's empty panning station read as built and fail. I fixed it by filling in that station. The other six were timeouts under parallel load, and each passed when run on its own.

**What's provisional:**
- **Rivers carry only what the local rock holds.** The mineral registry's "what the rivers carry" column doesn't exist yet: its numbers aren't approved and the registry mod isn't built. So for now a river carries gold, silver, jade or uranium only if that ore is in the map's own rock. Your ruling of river gold where the hills have none waits for the registry. There is one place to swap it in: `RM_PlacerDraw` in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\FlowWorks\Source\Quarry\RM_PlacerWorks.cs`.
- **One stock per map, not per river.** The design keeps a stock per river, but a map almost always has one river, so I used one per map.
- **Art is borrowed:** the sluice box uses the weir texture and the panning spot uses vanilla's meditation-spot mark. Real art still has to go through the art ledger.
- **All the numbers are first guesses.**

**Two things left uncommitted:**
- The `implemented` ledger event is only in `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry`'s `FOUNDRY.jsonl`, which already had other uncommitted edits. Per the brief I didn't touch that clone; that shard still needs committing from it.
- My selftest run changed `Transient/modcheck/fixtures.json` in my clone, and that blocked the pull. I saved the changed copy to `scratchpad/fixtures.selftest.json` and put back the committed version so the pull and push could go through.

Build notes and the live-proof checklist: `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\quarry_river_20261011.md`</result>

<result>I built Phase 2 (schemes as data and a compositor driving one mood) and pushed it as `d8007b690`. It has never been run in the game. The C# selftest passed 59/59, the mock selftest 103/103, the light-ledger check passed and the build is clean with no Harmony.

- **Not blocked:** Phase 2 drives only one mood, so it needs no rank. The rank work and the two blocking items only matter from Phase 3 on, and I touched neither.
- **Schemes:** a scheme is now an XML def, checked at load against the laws: at most 8 colours, no bright white (reserved for the Narrator), and full black only in a scheme that moves. It ships two: Lamplight, the gentle default, and Fireflies. Every colour stays bright enough to work under. Brightness is carried by the palette colours, so radius is never written.
- **Compositor:** one mood per map; a second push replaces the first. Fixtures only ever get a palette colour and are written only when their colour changes. Unpowered lights are skipped, and it runs on real time so it keeps going while paused. The driving call refuses any colour that is not in a loaded palette. With no mood set, Lamplight runs on the default group the player picks: whole ship, rooms, halls or outdoors.
- **Halls:** vanilla has no hallway room role, so a "hall" is any indoor room whose role is the generic `Room` or `None`. That is my own working rule, not something from the design doc.
- **Phase-1 gap closed:** when a gravship takes off and its map is removed, held fixtures used to fly away still showing the driven colour. Now each gets its own colour back when the map goes. A fixture's first evaluation after a reload also always writes once, so a reload can't leave it stuck on a driven colour.
- **Mod Settings:** three new toggles: animation on/off, default mood on/off, and where the default runs.
- **Still open:**
  - Neither live check has been run, `take_over_proof` nor the new `one_mood_proof`.
  - The item's end-of-Phase-2 cost re-measure on the owner's full mod list is also not done.
  - "Unpowered lights aren't driven" has no live test, because cutting power on his map would leave a trace.
  - Save/reload and leave-the-map release still have no live test.
  - The scenario-decides path is still API only, with no scenario part.
  - Unruled: the vanilla colour picker can still change a held light's colour. The next mood change overwrites it, and release restores the colour from before hand-over.

The `rimflow note` (marking the item doing) is written to the FOUNDRY ledger shard in `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry` but not committed. That file already had uncommitted changes from others, so I didn't commit it.

The Dakkra rest north and south renders are not done, so nothing was installed. The agent requeued both by hand, and they are pending again in `D:\Luke\dev\_artpipe\pending`.

The reason they kept failing was a stale failure record. `failed/ls_regen_RM_Dakkra_rest_v1_east.manifest.json` dated from the east render's first attempt on 10-05. Nothing was deployed or loaded, so the live check (L10) is still owed. Proof script tally is 7 PASS, 2 PARTIAL (L6, L9), 1 OWED (L10), 0 FAIL, and every new check went red on its planted defect.

- **L7, beskar smelting:** XML couldn't do it. Phase 2's `smeltable=false` on beskar also made beskar-made gear unsmeltable, and the stuff is per item, so an item def can't say "steel when made of beskar". `RSW_BeskarSmelt.cs` uses two Harmony postfixes, because the engine has no other hook here:
  - beskar gear can be smelted again (relics still can't);
  - smelting drops any beskar and gives steel at vanilla's 25% share of the beskar cost, plus one slag chunk. Those amounts are provisional.
  If the patch fails or its new Mod Settings toggle is off, beskar gear simply can't be smelted.
- **L1, Mines 2.0:** it builds its recipes in C# at startup, so no XML patch can reach them, and its own "don't create this" reader only looks at its own patches. `RSW_MineShaftCanonGate.cs` uses no Harmony: it runs Mines' generation first, then removes the banned recipes from the mine's bill list. It matches by product (beskar ×4, durasteel ×2, plasteel, doonium ore), so a second rock or extension recipe for the same material is caught too. Replayed on the live def dump, it removes 7 of 76 recipes and nothing else. Dropping Mines 2.0 isn't needed.
- **Not covered:** resources a player adds by hand in the Mines settings during play, and bills already on a mine in an old save (none exist yet).
- **Also built:** two new settings toggles, a `materials` family in the Armoury fuzz selftest (all families green), and the rebuilt DLL.

**Needs a ruling from the owner (not touched):**
- Mines 2.0 still offers shafts for industrial and spacer components, and for the Outer Rim hypertech component. That conflicts with his 2026-09-25 ruling that components are never mined. If he agrees, the fix is one line in the banned list.
- Mines 2.0 also lets gold and gems be mined on any map, against the home-biome rulings.

## Claude

The disagreement comes from one place: the seat clone `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry`. It's 244 commits behind origin/main, has 46 modified tracked files and 224 untracked ones, and carries 8 local commits. That stale base makes the ledger lint hook refuse every push from it. Everything else has been agents working around it through fresh clones and `ledger_sync.py`.

I checked what's in there before proposing anything.
- **Local commits:** the 8 are content-equivalent to what's already on origin. The only 3 whose patch-ids differ are agent commits (two decay sweep reports and one ledger note), whose reports and ledger lines were published through other commits.
- **Ledger lines:** all stranded ledger lines have been rescued. The last rescue run showed 0 missing, though agents have written a few more since.
- **Modified tracked files:** I haven't re-sorted the 46 into phantom and real. The earlier audit found them to be phantom or stale, with the ledger shard as the only real content, now rescued.
- **Untracked files:** the 224 are scratch (Transient, ModsConfig backups, conversation transcripts).

**Proposed fix: replace the clone, not reset it.** Resetting is what the hook forbids, and moving it aside is lossless.
1. Wait until no agents are running, so nothing writes mid-move.
2. Run `ledger_sync.py` one last time, so every ledger line reaches origin.
3. Rename `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry` to `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry_old_20261011`. This keeps all 224 untracked files and the 8 local commits.
4. Make a fresh clone at `/home/mandrake/rm/foundry`. On the ext4 drive it takes minutes. It starts at origin/main with a clean tree, so the hooks, `rimflow`, `./publish` and the pre-push lint work normally from then on.
5. Restart this session in the new directory. The rename takes the current session's working directory with it, so the session has to restart.
6. Later, delete the old copy, once you've seen nothing was missed.

**Prevention:**
- **Use `git pull --rebase origin main` often.** Nothing in my workflow did, and that's how 244 commits piled up.
- **Make the pre-push hook say "stale base: pull first".** Today it only prints a ledger-lint finding about missing lines, which reads like data loss.
- **Rotate the 32 MB art events log** so it doesn't drive more churn.
- **BENCH's clone has the same disease.** It's 18 behind, 6 ahead and 2,188 dirty. Your call whether to apply the same recipe there.

I'd do this at your next restart rather than overnight. **Do you want it done tonight, with agents paused first, or when you're at the keyboard?**

<note>This agent stopped with background work of its own still running. It may resume on its own when that work completes or reports, and the same task-id notifies again if it does; the result below may be interim.</note>
<result>I built and tested the rotation tool, but I did not rotate the live file. BENCH committed to it at 01:09 (`7cf08bf64`), which is inside your 60-minute quiet window. `BENCH.jsonl` is still 32,038,133 B. The dry run shows it would drop to 961,854 B live (1,595 lines), with 31,076,279 B moved to a new `archive/BENCH.0001.20261004_20261009.jsonl`. It is a pure move: no kept line is rewritten.

- **The command:** `art.py rotate &lt;SEAT&gt; (--before ISO | --keep-mb N) [--apply]`, a dry run unless you pass `--apply`. It moves the oldest part of the file, byte for byte, into archive files that are written once and never changed (30 MiB cap each). It always leaves at least 50 lines in the live file. It refuses if BENCH appends anything while it runs.
- **Readers updated:** the main event reader and its cache, the texture guard's git reader, `legacy_redo_triage.py` and `canon_check_labelset.py`. They now read each seat's archives first, then the live file. `.gitattributes` needs no change. `ledger_lint.py` never reads art events, so it won't block a rotation push.
- **Test:** `selftest_rotate.py` runs on a copy of the real files and passes 22 of 22 checks. Archives plus live file match the original exactly, and the folded state is the same after rotating by date and again by size. The texture guard's git reader gives the same result, and an older clone that appends and then merges ends up correct. The log rotation tool is built and pushed, but I have not rotated the live file, and I can't yet. That is inside the 60-minute quiet window, so `BENCH.jsonl` is still 32,038,133 B.

- **Command:** `art.py rotate <SEAT> (--before ISO | --keep-mb N) [--apply]`. It is a dry run unless you pass `--apply`.
- **What it does:** it moves the oldest part of the file, byte for byte, into archive files that are written once and never changed (30 MiB cap each). It always leaves at least 50 lines live, and it refuses if BENCH appends while it runs.
- **Dry run on BENCH:** the live file drops to 961,854 B (1,595 lines), and 31,076,279 B moves to a new `archive/BENCH.0001.20261004_20261009.jsonl`. No kept line is rewritten.

- **A trap to know:** anything that reads `events/*.jsonl` directly with a glob will now miss the archived events. The warning is in the design doc and the code.

<result>I wired the step-4 result into the recorded checkout and pushed it as `376783679`. The modcheck and FlowWorks northstar selftests pass 33/33. Nothing was run live (no bridge, no game, no `modcheck run`).

- **How it records:** `modcheck record FlowWorks --result &lt;core&gt; --extensions &lt;extension_result&gt;` now stores step 4 as a `step4` field in the same entry. A step-4 result is checked the same way as the core one, so it is refused if it is stale, on the wrong tier, aborted, or a partial `--chains` run.
- **Verdicts:**
  - A failing (or UNMEASURED) step-4 feature makes the checkout RED, the same as a failing core row.
  - With no step-4 result, step 4 is recorded as UNMEASURED and an otherwise-green core is REFUSED, with the reason naming step 4. It never reads as PASS.
- **Who reads it:** `modcheck status` and `modcheck floor --all` (which gains a `step4` column) both read it through one function, and only a stored PASS at the mod's current hash counts as PASS. That means any GREEN entry without a step-4 record, including a plain `modcheck run`, now shows as UNMEASURED.
- **Which mods it applies to:** a mod owes step 4 if it ships `northstar/extension_proof.py`. That script now writes the fields the record check needs into its result.
- **Selftests:** `selftest_record.py` has 49 checks, covering a planted failing step 4, a missing one, stale, partial and old-format results, and a legacy GREEN entry.

**Still owed:** criterion A2 (a live run). Every `extension_result_*.json` on disk was written before this change, so `--extensions` will refuse all of them. The next bridge pass has to run step 4 in full right after a core run, then record both together.

<result>I found no code defects in the five fixes, and filed one behaviour finding from P6a as `FLOOD_SWITCH_OFF_PROVE_SPILLS_GO_NOWHERE`. The report and the item are pushed in `92875a4de`.

**The finding (P6a, `57b4b4120`):** when "Spilled liquid fills channels properly" is switched off, a FlowWorks spill now places almost nothing — only over natural non-water liquid ground such as a tar pit. Spills can only spread into dug cells, and P6a now keeps them out of dug cells. Elsewhere the release just ends with its liquid unspent, so the tar-pit belch, Forge cycle and hot-liquid spills do nothing. The new settings text, "Off: a spill never enters a dug channel", reads as if spills still happen elsewhere. The default is on, so shipped behaviour is unchanged. I didn't fix it because the choice is a design call: either accept that off means no spills and correct the text, or give off a real meaning.

**Selftests on origin/main:** 17 of 17 FlowWorks selftests pass, including the C# selftest. I used the build folder `_rmbuild/wAI1`.

**Do the new checks go red on the old behaviour?** Yes. Checking out the parent files doesn't work, because they don't have the new functions and won't compile. Instead, in a throwaway copy I never committed, I reverted each new function in the production code to the old rule. Each of the five new checks then failed, and nothing else changed. Two caveats:
- The "old rule" probes written inside the tests (P5, P6a, P6b, P8) are copies of the old rule written into the test itself. They don't run the old production code, so they prove little on their own. The P4 test similarly re-writes the pump cycle rather than running the pump's own code.
- None of the places that call the fixes is compiled into the selftest: the pump, the drill, flood placement, tar cooling and the fire's burn pulse. Removing a call leaves everything green, so I checked those by reading the code.

**Engine facts:** RimSage confirms that a queued temp-terrain removal clears whatever temp terrain is on the cell, including the channel engine's own fill. So the race P6a fixes was real.

**Other checks that came out clean:**
- Save/load and existing saves: no new saved fields.
- The network-seams change (`4764c7143`): inputs from a pump or drill feed the order computed over the whole network.
- The quarry/sluice placer (`e569931e6`): it doesn't touch the channel engine at all.
- The lava row (`3f6213543`): only tar cools to glass, so the P6b path does nothing for lava or any other liquid.
- P5 debits liquid at the same unit per level the pump and drill use.

The review is at `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\flowworks_review_P4_P8_20261011.md` and the filed item is at `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\infrastructure\state\items\FLOOD_SWITCH_OFF_PROVE_SPILLS_GO_NOWHERE.md`. ## Claude

The independent review of the P4–P8 fixes found no code defects. It filed one behaviour finding from P6a as `FLOOD_SWITCH_OFF_PROVE_SPILLS_GO_NOWHERE`, with the report and item pushed in `92875a4de`.

**The finding.** When "Spilled liquid fills channels properly" is off, a FlowWorks spill now places almost nothing: only over natural non-water liquid ground such as a tar pit. Spills can only spread into dug cells, and P6a keeps them out of dug cells. Elsewhere the release ends with its liquid unspent, so the tar-pit belch, Forge cycle and hot-liquid spills do nothing. The new tooltip, "Off: a spill never enters a dug channel", reads as if spills still happen elsewhere. The default is on, so shipped behaviour is unchanged. **Is it acceptable that "off" means no spills, with the tooltip corrected, or should "off" keep a real meaning such as the old behaviour?** The agent left this because it's a design call.

**Tests:** 17 of 17 FlowWorks selftests pass on origin/main, including the C# selftest. Each of the five new checks went red when the agent reverted its function in a throwaway copy of the production code. Two caveats:
- **Inside the tests:** the "old rule" probes for P5, P6a, P6b and P8 are copies of the old rule written into the test. They don't run the old production code, so they prove little alone. The P4 test likewise re-writes the pump cycle.
- **Call sites:** the pump, the drill, flood placement, tar cooling and the fire's burn pulse are not compiled into the selftest. Removing a call leaves everything green, so the agent checked those by reading the code.

**Clean on every other check:** save/load and existing saves (no new saved fields); the network-seams change `4764c7143` (pump and drill inputs feed the order over the whole network); the quarry and sluice code `e569931e6` (doesn't touch the channel engine); the lava row `3f6213543` (only tar cools to glass, so P6b does nothing for other liquids); and P5 debits at the same unit per level the pump and drill use. RimSage also confirms a queued temp-terrain removal clears the channel engine's own fill, so the P6a race was real.

I started a new Opus agent on the weak spot. It adds call-site assertions for all the P-series fixes and shows each going red when a call is deleted in a throwaway copy. It doesn't change production behaviour.

The review is at `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\flowworks_review_P4_P8_20261011.md`, and the item is at `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\infrastructure\state\items\FLOOD_SWITCH_OFF_PROVE_SPILLS_GO_NOWHERE.md`.

<result>I built and pushed `TITAN_BREAKTHROUGH_CLEARING_1` at `541a7f731`: a titan now smashes through a lane that walls or giant plants seal off, instead of giving up. The offline tests pass, but nobody has seen it work in a running game yet.

**Why I picked it:** no Gimme Some Slack or FlowWorks item was buildable offline. Power theft needs the bridge, and the hose-reel move already landed today. This was the oldest offline item with a full spec, check and criteria.

**What it does:**
- **The cause:** the game rejects an unreachable destination before the titan takes a single step, so the titan never got near the wall it is supposed to crush.
- **The fix:** at that moment, a tiered titan now walks to the first obstacle in its way. It strikes it about once a second using the existing crush rules. It then picks up the job it was doing and walks through the gap.
- **What it may hit:** walls and buildings from T2, destroyed outright at T3. Giant plants only from the smash tier (T3 by default), with the damage going to the plant itself.
- **What it never hits:** natural rock, chunks, anything the crush table protects, quest objects, or buildings of its own or a friendly faction.
- **Long detours:** it also smashes through when walking round would take 2.5 times longer than going through. Short detours are walked.
- **Setting:** it has its own toggle under the wake, plus a slider for that detour ratio.
- **Giant plants:** the game's breach pathing ignores them (their trunk cells are only partly solid), so a straight-line check finds those instead.

**How it was checked:**
- The Huge Things fuzz passes, including four new test families. One simulates your check exactly: a lane sealed by a wall and a giant plant gets broken through, and a lane blocked by a protected building is never struck.
- As a sanity test, I removed the crush-table check on purpose. The lane test then failed 998 of 3000 cases with "a protected building was struck", and passed again once I put it back.
- The DLL builds with 0 errors and 0 warnings. `validation.py`, the two Huge Things lints and the settings-screen selftest all pass.

**Still owed:**
- **A2, the live walk:** it needs a T2 or larger race (the test Elephant is only T1 and crushes no walls). It is recorded as unmeasured in `validation.py`.
- **A1** is level-tagged L1, which rimflow says needs the bridge, so it is still owed even though the offline proof covers it.
- **Large titans:** the obstacle search works from the titan's centre cell, so a big titan might stop short of a one-cell gap. Unmeasured.
- **My call, please check:** a tame titan never smashes its own faction's buildings. The ruling says "anything built", so tell me if you want that rule lifted.

The item is now `built` in rimflow, owing A1 and A2.

Files are in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\HugeThings\` (once the mirror refreshes):
- `Source\Kernel\RM_TitanClearingKernel.cs`
- `Source\Titanic\Clearing\TitanClearingUtility.cs`

## Claude

The titan breakthrough is built and pushed at `541a7f731`. A titan now smashes through a lane that walls or giant plants seal off, instead of giving up. The offline tests pass, but nobody has seen it work in a running game. I've pushed the ledger events too (`e3de9bbec`).

The agent picked `TITAN_BREAKTHROUGH_CLEARING_1` because no GimmeSomeSlack or FlowWorks item was buildable offline. Power theft needs the bridge, and the hose-reel move already landed today.

- **The cause:** the game rejects an unreachable destination before the titan takes a step, so the titan never got near the wall it is supposed to crush.
- **The fix:** a tiered titan now walks to the first obstacle in its way, strikes it about once a second using the existing crush rules, then picks up its job and walks through the gap.
- **What it may hit:** walls and buildings from T2, destroyed outright at T3. Giant plants only from the smash tier (T3 by default), with the damage going to the plant itself.
- **What it never hits:** natural rock, chunks, anything the crush table protects, quest objects, or buildings of its own or a friendly faction.
- **Long detours:** it also smashes through when walking round would take 2.5 times longer than going through. Short detours are walked.
- **Setting:** its own toggle under the wake, plus a slider for the detour ratio.
- **Giant plants:** the game's breach pathing ignores them, because their trunk cells are only partly solid. A straight-line check finds those instead.

**Checks:** the Huge Things fuzz passes, including four new test families. One simulates your check: a lane sealed by a wall and a giant plant gets broken through, and a lane blocked by a protected building is never struck. With the crush-table check removed on purpose, the lane test failed 998 of 3000 cases, and passed again once it was back. The DLL builds with 0 errors and 0 warnings. `validation.py`, the two Huge Things lints and the settings-screen selftest pass.

**Still owed:** A2, the live walk, needs a T2 or larger race, because the test Elephant is only T1. A1 is tagged L1, which rimflow says needs the bridge. The obstacle search works from the titan's centre cell, so a large titan might stop short of a one-cell gap. That is unmeasured.

**Question for you:** a tame titan never smashes its own faction's buildings, which was the agent's call. The ruling says "anything built". **Do you want that rule lifted?**

Files are under `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\src\RimMandrake\HugeThings\`: `Source\Kernel\RM_TitanClearingKernel.cs` and the files in `Source\Titanic\Clearing\`. The notes are in `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\pickAJ_20261011.md`.

I started another picker. If the offline queue is exhausted, it says so with a count and stops rather than inventing work. Three agents are running: that picker, the call-site coverage agent, and the earlier general picker.

## Claude

I want you to look at the owner-said guard fix, because it loosens a safety control. Picker J fixed and closed `OWNER_SAID_GUARD_MIDTURN_BLIND_1` at `65de8e2bf`. I've pushed its ledger events (`fd3ad0e5a`).

- **Why that item:** every offline FlowWorks and GimmeSomeSlack item was waiting on the bridge, already built or validated, or built by BENCH today. This was the oldest open offline FOUNDRY bug, unclaimed with no commits.
- **The bug:** `.claude/hooks/block_forged_owner_said.py` refused your real quotes as forged whenever you typed them while an agent was mid-turn. It only read chat lines marked as typed. Text typed mid-turn is logged in two other shapes, which it read neither of. Across 528 saved transcripts there were 703 lines of the first shape and 661 of the second, all marked as coming from a human. Three lessons record this hitting real rulings (2026-09-24, 09-26, 10-04).
- **The fix:** both shapes now count, but only when marked as human. Messages relayed from other agents' sessions (136) and task notifications ride the same queue and are still refused.
- **Proof:** the selftest failed on the three new owner cases before the fix (24/27) and passes after (27/27). The agent replayed quotes from real transcripts through both versions. Owner lines typed mid-turn: old version accepted 0 of 20, new version 20 of 20. Lines relayed from other agents: refused 8 of 8 by both.

Because this guards against agents forging your authorization, I started an Opus agent to attack it. It feeds the guard synthetic transcript lines to see which are accepted: a fake "human" marker in a tool result, a subagent transcript line, a task notification quoting words, a Read of a transcript file, pasted content, and short-quote or normalization tricks. It tightens the guard if any attack gets through. It tests the script directly, not by exercising the hook live, because a hook added mid-session doesn't fire until a new session.

Notes are in `Transient/pickAM_20261011.md`.

Two agents are running: that review and the earlier general picker.

<result>I tried 37 attacks. The guard as of 65de8e2bf accepted 19 of them; after my fix it accepts 2, and neither can be fixed inside this hook. The fix is pushed as `ab1dab8b6`. The report is `D:\Luke\dev\RimMandrake\Transient\owner_said_guard_review_20261011.md` (skeleton pushed first as `f5400766a`).

**The change itself is sound.** The two new line shapes only count when the line is marked `origin.kind == "human"`, and only Claude Code writes that field. Nothing an agent writes or reads can produce it: tool results, file contents, web content, subagent reports, notifications and messages relayed from other sessions are all recorded differently. I checked every line in all 4,420 transcript files under `~/.claude/projects`. The commit let in one extra case, a queued human line flagged `isMeta`. No such line exists in the files, but I closed it anyway.

**The weakness was older than the commit: how a quote was matched.** A quote passed if it appeared anywhere, even mid-word, in all of his messages joined together. These all passed:
- a one-letter quote, or `"..."`, which reduces to nothing and therefore always matched;
- `"$A"`: the hook checked the letter `a`, while rimflow recorded whatever `$A` held;
- `--owner "…"`, which rimflow's parser treats as `--owner-said` (I confirmed this);
- `--sa`, a python argument list, and setting `RIMFLOW_OWNER_SAID=` directly;
- quotes stitched together across two of his messages, or across a card question we wrote;
- the validate-question-card hook's own refusal text, Claude Code's card text it didn't strip ("follow what they actually say"), and the boilerplate added when he rejects a card;
- text he pasted from somewhere else;
- dropping a "not" ("push to main" when he typed "do not push to main").

**What the fix requires now:**
- A quote must be whole words inside one of his messages.
- A one-word quote must be his entire message.
- The quote can't start just after "not", "never", "don't" and the like.
- `$` and backticks are refused unless the variable is set to a literal value in the same command, which agents really do (`Q="…"; … "$Q"`).
- Card results count only if they read as answers, plus his typed reason when he rejects a card.
- Pasted blocks are cut out.

The selftest is now 57/57. Every new attack case is red on the old guard, and every legitimate case from the harness still passes, including the two mid-turn shapes and the queued shape.

**What it costs.** I replayed all 572 real past commands that carried the flag. 23 would now be refused:
- About 9 are his real words that an agent stitched together from separate messages or card answers. Those refusals are correct; the agent has to quote one message.
- About 14 mention the flag in plain text followed by an ordinary word, like "from" or "for". The refusal message now says to wrap the flag in backticks.

**Subagent transcripts can't pass as his.** No line in any subagent file is marked the way the guard accepts. A subagent's brief carries no human marker. When he types to a subagent, those lines are refused too, which is the safe direction. One thing I couldn't check this session: which transcript file Claude Code hands the hook for a subagent's command.

**The two attacks still accepted:**
- **Indirection:** putting the flag in a script file, or setting the variable from inside python. The hook only sees the command text, so the check needs to move into rimflow, `broadcast.py` and `apply_blanket_ruling.py`.
- **Transcript tampering:** an agent appending a fake typed line to its own transcript file. A hook refusing writes to `~/.claude/projects/**.jsonl` would close it.

Two smaller gaps remain. A quote can still drop a condition at its end ("push to main" from "push to main after review"). And I found no examples of how scheduled wakeup prompts are recorded, so I couldn't test them.

37 attacks tried, 2 accepted (19 before the fix).</result>

<result>Pushed in 9add2e52a, 2f500a246 and 6b77a0a7d. Barbslinger and the Settings audit are done. Six of the review's C# files are now CLEAN. `validation.py` stays DIRTY with two findings open. Deploy is still owed.

- **(a) Barbslinger:** its item was already closed (owner kept the art 2026-10-06). The only new work is a lint check that the tail gun's `turretDef` points at a real def. A planted typo turns it red. Firing at range then closing to melee still needs a live test.
- **(b) startTick:** the hiding was not a bug. The engine sets and saves its own `startTick` and never reads it through the base class, so the copy ran alongside harmlessly. I removed the copy anyway and made member hiding a build error. Re-adding the field now fails the build.
- **(b) Real bug, fixed:** the burrow's one-day safety cap didn't work. When a fire never cleared, the animal went straight back underground. A timed-out burrow now waits 3 in-game hours before it can burrow again, and the fuzz test fails if that check is planted out.
- **(b)/(c) False settings text, corrected:** the "Mod enabled" tooltip said only fulgurites ignore it, but lightning breakers, sand shovelling and the restart-time switches do too. Comments called the cross-biome ash option worldgen-only, but it applies live. The static Settings audit passes 107/0; those two were its only findings.
- **CLEAN:** `RM_PyrelandsMod.cs`, `RM_JobGiver_BurrowOnFire.cs`, `RM_JobDriver_Burrow.cs`, `PyrelandsTuning.cs`, `PyrelandsMechanicsGate.cs` and the shell's `RM_BiomesMod.cs`, each after a full-file review with zero significant findings. The extension and DefOf files were already clean.
- **`validation.py`:** fixed the scorch-fruit cap check, which counted one small area instead of the whole map. Also fixed the breaker check, which cleared the map centre where the colonists stand. Still open:

## Claude

- `Agent — Pyrelands validation review findings`

## Claude

The Pyrelands `validation.py` fixes are pushed as `96893677f`, but the file is still DIRTY. A fresh full-file review by an Opus subagent found more significant issues, so I recorded no code review entry. No game or bridge was used, only offline probes.

- **Fulgurite:** the code places at most one fulgurite per strike, and one strike can light many fires. The check now counts checks-with-fire as a lower bound on strikes through a new `fulgurite_expected` function, with no fire sum. A probe showed the old formula demanded 8.75 fulgurite from one strike's 50 fires, against 0.175 now. The UNMEASURED cutoff went from 1 to 3 expected fulgurite. A working patch still shows zero about e^-expect of the time, so a lower cutoff could fail a mechanic that works.
- **Settings:** each setting's previous value is saved on first touch and put back in a `finally`, even after an earlier failure. This covers the ten chains that flip settings, and a fake-session probe confirmed the restore after a raised error. If a restore fails, the saved value is kept rather than cleared.

The review's still-open findings:
- **Cleanup after FAIL:** fire extinguishing and the weather unlock are skipped once a check has failed, so fires and locked weather can carry into later chains.
- **Fulgurite chain:** the strike bound is biased away from sand, other mechanics' fires inflate the count, the chain doesn't use the live `fulguriteChance`, and a fulgurite merging into an existing stack isn't counted.
- **Silent restore failure:** a failed settings restore only prints to stderr and doesn't change any verdict.
- **Read failures pass:** `scorchfruit_spoils` and `_fresh_site` can pass when their final read fails.
- **Lightning-breaker chain:** one component can poison the other, and the research gate check can pass without testing the live gate.

I started an Opus agent to fix all of them. It makes cleanup run in a `finally`, makes a failed restore or read mark the run non-PASS, and isolates the lightning-breaker components. It then does its own critical full-file review and marks the file clean only if that finds nothing significant.

Two agents are running: that one and the remaining owner-said wiring.

- There is no art-ledger repair tool.
- Still open from the last pass: a process launched outside the seat's cgroup looks like the owner's own terminal, and an agent can edit the verifier itself.

<result>`src/RimMandrake/Pyrelands/validation.py` is now **CLEAN**: all five findings are fixed and pushed (fix `a542f6539`, clean record `d93d8006e`). Nothing ran against the game.

1. **Cleanup after a FAIL:** the old cleanup calls did nothing once a check had failed. Each chain that lights fires, locks weather or flips settings now puts out fires, unlocks the weather and restores settings through the session directly, so it runs even after a FAIL.
2. **Fulgurite chain:**
   - The other fire mechanics are switched off before the storm, so their fires can't be counted as lightning strikes.
   - It reads the live `fulguriteChance` instead of assuming 0.35.
   - It counts fulgurite by summed stack size, so one that merges into an existing stack is counted.
   - Sand is measured against all non-water cells.
   - The strike count is still "windows with any fire". I tried counting fire clusters and dropped it: a burning animal that runs off would make one strike look like several.
   - On sand bias: strikes land uniformly at random, so fewer fires on bare sand can only make the expected count lower, never higher. The script's docstring states this.
3. **Restore failure:** a teardown step that fails now adds a FAIL component, `teardown`, so the run cannot be all-green. The 33 declared components and their ids are unchanged.
4. **Final reads:**
   - `scorchfruit_spoils` now checks separately whether the stack is still on the map and whether it rotted. If the stack is present but its rot text can't be read, the verdict is UNMEASURED, not PASS.
   - `_fresh_site` treats a refused or partial terrain read as UNMEASURED.
5. **Lightning breaker:**
   - Each component now runs regardless of the one before. A surprise abort or a map the runner couldn't make bland still ends the chain.
   - The research gate check reads the live gate: `jawa/research_availability` with `breakerPyrelandsOnly` forced off, then on.
   - It reports UNMEASURED when vanilla already refuses the project or the project has progress, since the gate exempts begun research.
   - I applied the same independence fix to `startup_and_def_wiring`, `ullai_and_giant` and `sand_shovel`, which had the same problem.

- The fake-session probes score 24/24 on the fixed file and 7/22 on the old one, with every finding red there.
- Five planted defects each turned their own probe red.
- `selftest_pyrelands_site.py` passes, `modcheck/selftest.py` is 22/22, and `floor --all` shows the Pyrelands bar met.

My fresh full-file review found two more problems: the fire-cluster strike count could overcount, and the independence reset would have revived a chain on a non-bland map. Both are fixed, and nothing significant remains.

Two older points remain, neither significant:
- `burn_line_present` passes on any fire on the map, which is how that check is designed.
- The fulgurite FAIL threshold still allows about a 5% false FAIL.

Notes are in `Transient/pickAT_20261011.md`. The probe harness is `probe_pyre.py` in the scratchpad and is not committed.</result>

  - The strike count stays "windows with any fire". Counting fire clusters was tried and dropped, because a burning animal that runs off would make one strike look like several.
- **Restore failure:** a teardown step that fails now adds a FAIL component, `teardown`, so the run cannot be all-green.
- **Final reads:** `scorchfruit_spoils` checks separately whether the stack is still on the map and whether it rotted. If the stack is present but its rot text can't be read, the verdict is UNMEASURED. `_fresh_site` treats a refused or partial terrain read as UNMEASURED.
- **Lightning breaker:** each component now runs regardless of the one before it. The research gate check reads the live gate through `jawa/research_availability` with `breakerPyrelandsOnly` forced off, then on. It reports UNMEASURED when vanilla already refuses the project or the project has progress, since the gate exempts begun research. The same independence fix went into `startup_and_def_wiring`, `ullai_and_giant` and `sand_shovel`.

The fake-session probes score 24/24 on the fixed file and 7/22 on the old one. Five planted defects each turned their own probe red. `selftest_pyrelands_site.py` passes, `modcheck/selftest.py` is 22/22, and `floor --all` shows the Pyrelands bar met. Notes are in `Transient/pickAT_20261011.md`.

Two older points remain, neither significant: `burn_line_present` passes on any fire on the map, by design, and the fulgurite FAIL threshold still allows about a 5% false FAIL.

The seat clone is 11 commits behind origin and has nothing local to push. Only the art ledger repair tool is still running. It's 05:52, and I'll start one more offline picker as long as there's offline work left.
